- Bug-inbox mod (no app version): `.claude/skills/bug-inbox/`, a third Claude Code mod: every chat's first message carries the open `auto-bug` issues (counts by kind, hand-filed / crash / stuck ones listed, new ones since the last chat flagged for a one-line mention), a 30-min re-check passes on newly filed hand reports, `/inbox` lists all. Checked on the live list (82 open, all titles parse, note under 900 characters). 4 tests.
- v249: three smarter things that run by themselves (the owner picked 1, 2 and 4 from a list of ideas: "nah i need more smarter and useful"). All in the last script; switches in Settings → Extra features (`S.btOff`, `S.dwOff`, `S.qcOff`). (1) Buy advice (`smBuy`): `smReach()` walks the queue in On deck order (`smQueue`) at your real weekly hours (`smWeekly`: timer sessions of 6 weeks, else the pace on the games you're playing, else the evening setting) → "you'll get to it in ~N days"; checked against the store's big yearly sales (`SM_SALES`, approximate windows per store), the sale it's in now (`p.end`) and the lowest price seen (after 2+ weeks of watching): Buy now / Great price (−60% or lowest seen at −40%+) / Wait for <sale> / No rush / Skip the pre-order / Wait for the reviews. Shown under the prices (`pxCard` wrapper, `.smv`), the Shopping plan sheet (`smPlan`), and Nexi says it by himself when a game turns buy (`smWatch` after each price run + 32s after open; last verdict per game in localStorage `gq:bt`, a game is announced at most every 14 days) or when a price drop isn't worth it yet. "I own it" (`g.own`) hides it and stops the price checks (`pxItem` wrapper); the Prices section then says "You own it". (2) Drifting games (`dwRisk`): play days from timer sessions, logged-hour changes (now kept in `g.lgH`, last 40, by a `save` wrapper) and Where-was-I notes; drifting = gap since last played > 2.2x your usual gap for that game (and 4+ days, under the 14 where Still playing? takes over) plus one more sign (sessions halved, gaps widening, at your usual drop point = median progress of dropped games, kept from now on in `g.dropP`, or most games of its genre dropped). Card at the end of Your progress (`gdProgress` wrapper, `.dwc`) + one question (`#fnask`, once a day, per game every 3 weeks): Something short in between (`dwCleanse`: out, ≤12h or 40% of its length, least alike; finishing it → Nexi: "Back to X?" with your last note, via `g.dwBack`) / Park it / I'm into it. Needs logged hours, the timer or notes to see anything. (3) Queue clean-up (`qcCands`): queue games out 60+ days, hype under 8: waiting 6+ months unstarted, Nexi's guessed score (rvFor / expected) a point under your average, alike (Jaccard ≥ .5) to games you dropped, ≤1/3 duels won, or 18+ months away at your pace; score ≥3 with 2+ reasons. Monthly Nexi line (`S.qcAt`) when there are 3+; the sheet (`qcShow`) says why per game: Let go (like the swipe-delete, Undo while the sheet is open) / Keep (`g.qcK`, not again for 6 months). Tests (scratchpad h/: seed.js with 16 games covering every verdict, t1 logic, t3 14 real-touch checks, t4 tour 412/900, sweep.cjs 412/859/360 = 0 reports, plant2 proves a cut-off `.smv` line is caught; note: the checks wait ~4-6s after a screen opens, a sweep reading sooner proves nothing). Perf 4x, 195 games: game page markup 1–5ms (same as v248), render ≈ same, smWatch 8ms (+35ms for Nexi's bubble, as any line), qcCands 40ms (monthly / on open of the sheet).
- v249, part 2: smoother everywhere (the owner: "all tabs and windows lag", "a lot of lags that even Nexi doesn't see", asked if turning it into an app would fix it: no, a wrapper runs the same code in the same engine). Measured (perf.cjs: 196 games, 120 with canvas-free generated 600x900 covers + 3840x2160 art served from scratchpad h/img, LoAF + rAF per action, 4x CPU): the biggest job on every screen was Nexi's own problem check (`bugLayout` chain, 100–350ms at 4x after every tab switch / sheet / game page, rerun on screens checked a minute before, and its idle callbacks had a 3s timeout so they ran mid-movement). Now an outer wrapper: waits for a real idle moment (callback not timed out), checks each screen once a day until it changes (key = screen + window size + element count, localStorage `gq:bugdone`), marks done only if the base check ran (`BUG.lt === 0`). `lookTilt` (35% of a check: every text asked getComputedStyle for all its parents) remembers each element for one pass. Result at 4x: Settings 373 → 0–200ms (first visit only), Queue scroll 435 → 0, game page open 160 → 0, repeat visits 0. Nexi sees smaller lag: long frames from 100ms (`LAG_MIN`, was 150), scroll hitches > 40ms (was 60; reported at 3+ or 90ms+), new `lagWatch` for the 0.9s after goTab / openFull / closeFull / openSettings / dyShow (frames > 45ms after the first; 3+ with one 60ms+, or one 90ms+; message "Switching to Queue stuttered (…)"), and startup (LoAF buffered, frames ≥ 300ms in the first 8s → "Opening the app froze for Nms", once a day). Sweep 412/360: 0 reports; 859 alone: "Switching to Queue stuttered (2 hitches, worst 170ms)" which is real (157ms frames at 1x with no script: drawing/decoding the 4K art), left for the next round (and now reported from the phone). Planted cut-off text on a fresh screen is still caught.
- v250: "picked for you" previews stuck on "Getting screenshots and the full story…" (the owner, screenshot of a Discover card, 18 of 24: "not all games have screenshots inside here, fix"). Cause: `gpPaint` fetches the next game ahead (`gpLoad(nx, -1)`); opening it with › while that ran made `gpLoad` return (busy), and the ahead-load's end only redrew for `tok > 0 && tok === GP.tok`, so it never redrew. Now it redraws whenever the loaded game is the one on screen (preview open). Each lookup also has a time limit (sgResolve 10s, RAWG 12s, helper /art 15s; one that never answered meant "Getting…" forever), and a game with nothing found says "No story turned up for this one." Nexi learned it: a loading line (Getting/Loading/Checking/Asking/Fetching/Looking/Finding/Searching…/is thinking…) in `.gp-wait, .dy-empty, [class*=wait]`, visible, online, unchanged for 25s → kind "stuck", one per screen per day (5s interval). Tests (scratchpad h/): gp.cjs (fake /art answering in 3s, › at 1.5s): v249 stuck with 0 shots, v250 3 Steam shots; stuck.cjs: /art never answering → "No story turned up" at ~15s with no report, a planted "Getting…" line reported after 25s; sweep 412/859/360 incl. the preview + 35s dwell: 0 stuck/layout reports; tour 412/900.
- v251: the owner (screenshot of the unfolded rail with the queue covers circled): "change them into something useful with live animation that is smooth buttery and doesn't end" + "Nexi needs to get smaller to fit the border of the rail". (1) The rail's `.games` covers are replaced by a live strip (`rlv*`, last script, wraps `pnRail`; the "QUEUE" label is now "LIVE" with a soft dot): tiles from `rlvItems()` = playing games (ring = logged/hrsOf, "Nd left" from `etaOf`), the next 3 dated releases (countdown, hours on the last day), watched games on sale (best % off, `pxEff`/`pxIncl`), On deck #1, the rest of the ranked queue (#n) up to 9, then backlog hours (taps to Games). It drifts up at 15px/s forever: the tile list is copied enough times to cover the rail + 1 (sizes from a ResizeObserver, never read after the redraw) and one Web Animation moves the track by exactly one copy, `iterations: Infinity`, so there's no seam; a redraw keeps its place (progress carried over). Measured: same layouts/style recalcs with it running or paused (compositor only), frames steady. Touch holds it, a drag scrubs it (`currentTime`, never counts as a tap), it carries on 2s after you let go; tiles use `data-rg` so the rail's own click opens the game. Off (single copy, scrollable) in `still` / reduced motion and while the rail is hidden. Refresh: every `pnRail` call, once a minute, on coming back. (2) Seated Nexi fits the 72px rail: children scale .72, aura inset -7px (was -16px: 94px), custom picture inset -6px, glow inset -4px, rail padding-bottom 84px.
- v252: the card (the owner sent a clip of a game where a black card is handed over: its logo glitches into "[GREETINGS]", a slash crosses it and a triangle pattern takes over; "could we create something like this for our app?", then picked all three places offered). `pcdHTML(o)` builds it (CSS animations only, transform/opacity, delays scaled by `--k`): logo glitch → letters typed in → hairline → accent slash (hidden outside its run, `--sa/--sb` per shape) → triangle grid (8×4 landscape / 5×9 upright cells, two triangles each, ~60% filled white/grey/accent, swept in left to right); `.pcdrv` then flies the triangles off and shows `.pcd-face` (art + kicker + title); `.pcdst` = still. It lives in a small script right after `#pnboot` (the intro needs it before the rest loads) and its CSS replaced the v144 intro block in the first `<style>` (the v169 `#pnboot i` bar override is gone). (1) Launch intro: the card at `k .7`, out at 1350ms (was 1050) with an opacity fade (the old clip-path exit is gone), tap skips, off with motion off as before. (2) Calling card (`callingCard`): the PNG arrives as an upright card (`ar .5625`, Reshuffle plays it at `k .6`); calm → the old still picture. (3) Release-day card (last script, `pcdDue`/`pcdInvite`/`pcdTry`): replaces the old automatic OUT NOW screen (`outNow` now returns unless `S.pcdOff`) and shares its "already shown" list `S.fxSeen`; due = dated games out within 14 days, not played/dropped/competitive/playing, not added after release, not opened since release (`gq:seenG`); one at a time (Next / Later, Open it → game page), waits for the intro, the daily calendar (`#fxcal`), a pending/running tour, a sheet or another card (every 3s, up to 3 min; again on return to the app). Settings → Extra features → Release-day cards (On/Off/Show me; Off brings OUT NOW back). Class clash caught while testing: `.rv` (and `.st`) were taken (a navy box appeared behind the card) → `.pcdrv`/`.pcdst`; the busy check uses `body > .ccov` because Home's calendar tile has an `<i class="ccov">`. Tests (scratchpad h/: t1 intro/invite/calling-card frames + reload at 412/900, t2 tour 412/900 + Show me + switch, t3 4x CPU: card build 4–7ms, 1–2 frames over 34ms at insertion, calling card's long frame is the existing canvas draw (same with the old still path), t4 auto-reports at 412/859/360: none from the card (only "never got its screenshots", the test has no internet), t6 no older rule reaches the card but button styles); the WebKit check caught `new Set(S.fxSeen)` throwing on its seed `fxSeen: 1` → `pcdShown()` accepts only a list, and webkitcheck.mjs now plays the release-day card and a calling card on iPhone/iPad).
- v252, part 2: the WebKit check had been red on iPad since v251 ("ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered notifications"): the live strip's ResizeObserver (`rlvFill.ro`) rebuilt the strip and re-observed inside its own callback. The rebuild now waits for the next frame; sizes are still read from the entries. Strip checked looping after resizes at 900/1100×700/860×1200.
- v253 (#338, a slow tap on the Games view switcher, Replay → Queue, 490ms on the Fold): `slidePill` read `offsetWidth` right after `goSub` redrew the Queue, so the tap laid out the whole new list (every queue row is drawn since #89). It now measures in the next frame (`slidePillNow` does the work; the rebuild path still calls it directly inside its rAF so the marker's no-transition first placement holds). 4x CPU, 250 games, 859×847: switching to Queue tap→paint ~450 → ~365ms, click handler ~100 → ~27ms, worst frame ~210 → ~142ms. Marker lands exactly on the pressed button at 412/900 (after switches, tab changes, a resize). The rest is the Queue's own style/paint/layerize with a long list (and ~14ms commits per frame from the running animations at 4x), left alone. Tests: scratchpad tsw.cjs (switch timings, N= games, ROOT= build), ttrace.cjs (trace), tpill.cjs (marker), tourat2.cjs SHOT=1 LV=252 (tour 412/900).
- v254: the card now lands in your hand, first person (the owner: "you see the video I sent, a hand holds the card then the card moves, you know, first person, I really wanted the same effect"). `PCD_HAND` (SVG in the early script by `#pnboot`): a white-gloved right hand seen from behind, thumb on the card's face pinching its lower-right corner, fist below, dark sleeve and shirt cuff off the bottom-right corner (three drawing passes in a scratchpad prototype: thumbs-up stick → sausage fingers → one fist with soft creases). `pcdHTML` now nests `.pcd-rig` (hand + card, `pcdRig`: up from the bottom right, narrow and leaning, swings open to face you with a small overshoot, .95s) → `.pcd-sway` (one slow sway) → `.pcd-card` + `.pcd-hand`; the card's own timeline moved +.45s so it starts once it faces you. `.pcdrv`: the hand lowers out (`pcdHo`) and the card comes to the middle (`pcdRigF`) before the triangles fly off. The group sits 13% left so the hand fits. Release-day card: the game's art is the world behind (`.pcdov-bg`, dim, slow push-in). Intro at k .6, out at 1450ms. Calling card: upright card held at its lower corner (`.ccov .pcd-hand`). Perf (all measured): (1) the first try used real 3D (`perspective` on `.pcd`, rotateY): 1.4s first-play freeze at 4x (the 60 triangle pieces waiting inside the turning 3D card; `.pcd-tri` now stays `visibility:hidden` until 1.6s) and, unfolded (859), 15–20fps for the card's whole stay even after the 3D was switched off or moved into the keyframes only (software compositing in the test; 7–13 "stutter on Home" self-reports vs 0–1 for v252). The turn is now 2D (scale + skewY), which reads the same in side-by-side frames: 0 self-reports at 412/859/360, 4x CPU release card 1 frame over 34ms (v252: 1), intro 0–3. (2) Found while checking the calling card: Home's calendar rule `html.ui2 .ccov {display:block; position:absolute; inset:4px; border-radius:50%}` (and `.ccov {display:none}` for Arcade) also hit the calling-card and share-card overlays, so they've shown as a navy oval at the top of the screen (or not at all in Arcade); scoped to `i.ccov`, and their clean-up `querySelectorAll(".ccov")` (which also deleted the calendar's covers) to `body > .ccov`.
- v255: the card flies at you from far away and plays a lot (the owner, after v254's glove: "remove the hand, the card comes to you from far, first it's small then it gets to you, and then the card animates a lot, like a lot"). The hand is gone (`PCD_HAND`, `.pcd-rig/-sway/-hand` removed, v254's tour step replaced by v255's). `pcdHTML` now builds a scene in one grid cell: `.pcd-spark` (a distant light where it comes from), `.pcd-lines` (14 speed lines streaking out), three `.pcd-gh` ghosts a beat behind, then `.pcd-fly` (`pcdFly`, .72s: tiny and spinning 1.2 turns, edge-on twice via scaleX, growing) → `.pcd-shk` (`pcdImp`: lands 16% too big, recoils, shakes; `pcdPunch` at 2.62s) → `.pcd-flip` → `.pcd-card`. Landing: two rings (`.pcd-ring`, volt + white) and a glow burst (`.pcd-glow`). On the card: RGB-split logo glitch (two coloured copies `.c1/.c2`), scanline twice (`.pcd-scan`), letters drop in with a bounce, hairline, two slashes (volt, then a thin white one the other way, `.s2`), triangles sweep in with a spin and a pulse wave (`pcdTi`, .9s), punch. `.pcdrv`: flips (`pcdFlip` at 3.4s) — cover and triangles go at the midpoint — to show the game, shine sweep (`.pcd-shine`), text, note 3.9s, buttons 4s. Intro at k .55, out at 1850ms. All 2D transform/opacity (v254's 3D lesson). Perf: the sweep (t4, now tapping only once the buttons are up) first showed 110-280ms stutters at the release card's reveal and 400-830ms around the calling card; switching parts off found the flip: squeezing the card to exactly 0 wide; at .06 (still reads edge-on) every sweep is clean at 412/859/360 (2 runs each width while testing), and the flip waits for the triangles' wave to finish (3.4s, was 2.82: 250-370ms frames at the old time). 4x CPU: release card 2 frames over 34ms (v254: 1), intro 1, calling card same as the canvas draw alone. WebKit check: the first run's iPhone 11 page crashed twice around the new card step; a temporary hunt (fresh iPhone 11 page, release card + calling card + reshuffle, 9 variants × 2) ran 17/18 clean, "as built" 2/2, the one crash at a random point in a variant without the flip: the runner flake webkitcheck.mjs already describes. The full walk re-run passed with no crash on any device.
- Detector-only fix after v255 (no version; #354 #355 #356 #358, all false alarms; three came from one wake-up after ~40 min asleep): (1) `bugModal` (shared by every lag check) treats a 10s+ frame as the phone asleep (#355 read "A 2395030ms stutter"). (2) A new last script adds "quiet windows" to `BUG_MODAL`: each resize (+0.8s) and each time the app was hidden/frozen until 1.5s after it's back (visibilitychange, freeze/resume, pagehide/pageshow); overlapping windows merge, list capped at 16. v245's `LAG.rz` kept only the latest resize, so during a fold's burst of resizes the first one's frames went out as "DOMWindow.onresize" stutters (#354, #358), and the 400ms longtask freeze check never skipped resizes. (3) Cloud backup counts a failure toward its 3-strike report only while the app is on screen and has been back 15s+ (`cbShownAt`; #356 "Failed to fetch" right after waking). Test (scratchpad tquiet.cjs, 4x, resizes 411↔859↔600 every 0.4s, a fake freeze/resume around a 600ms busy frame, a 10.5s busy frame, then a real 450ms freeze): before 10 + 2 + 1 false reports, after 0 + 0 + 0; the real freeze is still reported in both.
- v256 (#352, "A 160ms stutter on a game page", blamed on a wsrv `?w=600` picture's onload, 108ms blocking on the Fold): it was the calling card. `callingCard` drew its 1080×1920 canvas in one go right after the cover loaded (an `await` continuation is reported as that image's onload), while the "Making your calling card…" toast came in. Now it yields a frame (`yf`: rAF then setTimeout) after the cover loads, after the background, after the "Calling card" letters, after the pinned cover and after the title letters. 4x CPU (scratchpad tcard.cjs): the drawing's longest frame 135–150ms cold / 47–73 warm → ≤45ms; the remaining 50–100ms frames are the card's own entrance (pcdHTML), the same before and after. Same seed gives byte-identical PNGs before/after (tcardhash.cjs). Tour 412/900 (points at the 🃏 Calling card button in the ⋯ drawer); step scan 160/160 at 900. #351 (a resize stutter from the same wake-up) closed under the earlier detector fix.
- v257: live Home background (the owner: "a new animation, 120 frames per sec or higher", picked "Live Home background" from four options). Last `<style>`/`<script>`, all `hlv*`; `#hlv` (fixed, z 0) is inserted after `#bgtint` and shows only on `html.onhome.hlvon`. `hlvSync` (wraps `renderHome` as `_rh7`, runs in rAF) reads the hero's `data-home-open` (an attribute, no layout), takes `bgSource(g) || cover(g)` and, when it changed, `hlvBuild` loads the light copy (`liteFor`/`blobRef`, CORS first so colours can be read, plain on refusal), draws it into a 72px-wide canvas (`.hlv-art`, Ken Burns drift 48s) and makes three 64×64 radial-light canvases (`.hlv-l1..3`, 31/41/53s closed loops, random negative delays) from the art's three strongest hue bins (`hlvColours`; falls back to `g.colour`, then `--vrgb`). 14 rising specks (`.hlv-d i`, three keyframe paths). Tiny canvases on purpose: the compositor stretches the texture, so nothing is re-rastered and every frame is compositor-only (trace: 0 BeginMainFrame/Paint/Layout with it on, same as off), i.e. it runs at the panel's refresh rate (120Hz on the Fold, 60 on the iPhone 11). A new #1 crossfades in a new `.hlv-s` scene (.9s opacity) and removes the old. `.dxw` goes translucent (rgba .72) while on. Paused under `#full.on` (`:has`), `dragging-now`, and while hidden; `html.still`/reduced motion leave it static. Switch: Home → Customise → Live background (`#s-hlv`, `S.hlvOff`, GROUP_KEYS "Live background"). Tested 412/859/360 (0 detector reports), tour at 412/900. A full-screen vignette layer was tried and dropped (one less full-screen blend per frame).
- v258: the live background on Games, Plan, Taste and every game's page (the owner, after v257: "where else", picked "Other tabs" + "Game page light"). Same engine, generalised: `hlvScene(box, o)` keeps up to ~4 hidden scenes per box keyed by `data-sig` (src|k|fast|cols), so going back to a screen just re-shows its scene; hidden scenes are `display: none` (no animations, no layers). `hlvWhat()` decides per tab: Home = the hero (S.hlvOff); Games = `focusId` or top of `sortGames(liveGames())`, `noart` when `#bgart` has a src (the queue's own backdrop already shows it); Plan = the next dated release, `k` .58→1 and lights 0.6× duration within 7 days (`fast`), else top of queue at .55; Taste = best-scored played game (else queue top) with lights from `hlvGenreCols()` (genre weights: played score, else hype/2; `HLV_GEN` regex → colour, unknown genres hashed to a hue). Non-Home screens are softer (k ≤ .75) since no panel covers the light. Triggers: wrappers on `renderHome`, `goTab`, `applyBg`, plus a MutationObserver on `#full` (childList + class). Game page: `#hlvg` is appended (last, `z-index: -1` so it draws under `.hub`) into `#full` after every redraw; first version prepended it and the WebKit check (iPad) reported "A game page flickers when it opens" (`flickWatch` measures `#full.firstElementChild`, which was the still-hidden light layer; Chrome's idle wait pushed the reveal past the 750ms window, Safari has no requestIdleCallback) (`hlvGame`, reads `hubAt`), `#full.hlvgon .hub.gpage` goes rgba .72 and `.gd-hero::after` fades to the same alpha (no seam); built 650ms + idle after the page opens. `#hlv` is `visibility: hidden` under `#full.on` (was only paused, still blended under the page). Switches: `S.hlvX` {games, game, plan, taste} in each screen's Customise → Live background (`#s-hlvx-*`). Measured: steady state 0 dropped frames on Taste/game page even in the GPU-less container; on Plan a pre-existing ~34Hz main-frame pipeline adds ~0.2ms style per frame with the light on; open/close transitions at 859 report hitches in the container with it off too. Also fixed (found while testing the tour): the Plan calendar's cover dots `i.ccov` took the calling card overlay's `.ccov { z-index: 9000 }` and floated over game pages opened from Plan; `i.ccov { z-index: 1 }` also lets the day number (v254's white shadowed `.cday`) show on the cover as designed. Not a detector case (pointer-events none, only visible in a screenshot).
- v259: the game page light was hard to spot (the owner: "the color thing in the game page I can't spot it"). Two causes: `hlvGame` only ran for `.hub.gpage` (New style), so Showcase (`.hub`) and Plain (`.hub.plain`) pages had none; and on New the `.72` panel let ~10% through. Now any `#full .hub` gets it (their `.hub` is already transparent over `#full`'s background), `k` 1.3 (was .9), New's panel `rgba(10,14,27,.5)` and `.gd-hero::after` fades to the same .5. Detectors clean on all three styles at 412/859 (flicker check run without requestIdleCallback, like Safari).
- v259, part 2: three new looks from the owner's references (a clinic site's row of tall solid-colour cards with a person standing in each, a "mr." court playing card, and two travel cards: full photo with a blurred bottom + full-width button, and photo-on-top + dark button + heart), "clearly visible, not hidden like calling cards". Last `<style>`/`<script>`, prefixes `dzp`/`dzd`/`dzc`, switches `S.dzX` {play, deck, court} (Customise: Home "Playing now cards", Games → Queue "On deck cards", Taste → Insights "Player type card"; New / Classic). (1) Home "Playing now": `dzPlay()` (wraps `renderHome` as `_rh8`) swaps `.dxplay .row3` for `.dzp-row`: cards in `color-mix(var(--tint) 50%, dark)`, label (Playing · N% / genre), big title, ↗ circle, the game's figure `g.key` bottom-right or its hero/cover masked into the lower 60%, thin progress bar (transform scaleX). Swipe row on phones, 3 across at 700px+. (2) On deck: `onDeckStrip` wrapper returns `.deck.dzd`: #1 `.dzd-a` full picture + a blurred copy (`filter: blur(14px)`, static, masked to the bottom) + "Play this next" pill + #1 glass chip; #2/#3 `.dzd-s` picture on top, accent "Open" pill + #n circle (the heart became the rank). Meta: ≈ hours, platform, out date. (3) Taste → Insights: `tasteInsightsPage` wrapper rewrites the `ix-hero` section (medal + shine out, words kept) to a court card: corner index (first 3 letters + "."), crowns (SVG, three trefoils on an arc, mirrored), the type's initial twice (striped text fill + stroke, second rotated 180°) with its emoji in the middle, patterned card backs either side; `.ix-hero.dzc` is a centred column (the hero is a 2-column grid). Load order gotcha: Home, the queue and Taste are drawn before the last script exists, so it redraws them once; the queue must be redrawn through `renderQueue()` (its `qPatch` cache skips a header that matches the last one: an `outerHTML` swap made the Classic switch do nothing). Detectors clean at 412/859/360; lag notes identical with the looks off.
- v259, part 3 (WebKit check): with the three looks, iPad portrait twice reported "Some text is too faint to read" on Home's Nexi line (`.nxl-g`, 2.4:1 then 1.8:1), never before v259 and never in Chromium (dry-run of webkitcheck.mjs, also with requestIdleCallback removed like Safari: clean). Steady state is ~6.5:1 (volt on the opaque `.nxh-say`), so the text was measured part-faded. Not pinned down: `webkitcheck.mjs` now wraps `bugReport` and prints "What made text faint" (text colour, html classes, every parent with opacity < 1, a background or running animations) under each device, so the next sighting explains itself. The next two runs were clean (one re-run after a known "Target page closed" runner crash).
- v260: smoothness + battery (the owner: "a lot of lag", "why isn't the whole app on fixed 120 fps", "regular apps are smooth… I don't want the app to be a monster of battery and RAM"). Phone reports #363 #365 #367 #368 (v258, unfolded): 110–260ms frames on Library/game page with tiny script, i.e. rendering. Measured (idle.cjs/sw.cjs/after.cjs/rest.cjs in the scratchpad: 40 games, 859×847 @2.63, 4x CPU; old build served as old.html from origin/main): (1) a spark burst on EVERY tap (FX pack click handler, full-screen canvas redrawn + re-uploaded each frame for ~1s, right when the next screen draws; also against #309 no press effects): now only the duel win keeps its sparks. Commit time over 4 tab switches 2247ms → 333ms. (2) `seatOn` read `innerWidth` from a MutationObserver on <html> class changes, forcing a full layout on every tab switch (71ms/8 switches unthrottled): width cached in `seatVW` (resize). (3) `paintTab` forced `void p.offsetWidth` before showing a panel (45ms/8): removed (display:none → block restarts the fade by itself). (4) Full-screen layers stacked every frame: `#pnorbs` and `html::before` hidden while the live light is on (`html.hlvon`), `#bgtint` only with an art backdrop (`html.bgon`, set by an `applyBg` wrapper), live lights 120vmax → 76vmax (~1 screen instead of ~3). (5) Rest: 12s without pointer/key/wheel/scroll, every running infinite animation is paused (`REST`, re-checked every 5s, `window.pnRest`), except quick "still working" signs (`REST_KEEP`: pnSpin/gDot/ncDot/pnShim under 2s); any touch, key, scroll or coming back wakes them. Also at rest: the countdown digits change without the flip, Nexi's power-up (a bolt + 3 embers every 180ms) can't start. Frames drawn per 2s at rest: 240 → 24–28 on Home/Queue/game page. The countdown flip lost its `filter: blur` (main-thread repaint each frame; speed rule). Switch: Settings → Motion → Rest when idle (`S.restOff`). Total long-frame time over 9 screen switches 2302–2493 → 1625–2098ms. Not done (measured, parked): `content-visibility: hidden` for hidden panels (only 10–18% better, risky). Detectors clean at 412/859/360 incl. a rest + wake cycle.
- v261: smoothness, round 2 (the owner after v260: "still lags a lot … I want 120 fps"). Measured with scratchpad harnesses (40 seeded games with PIL-made covers/heroes/logos/key art, 859×847 @2.63, 4x CPU; old build served as old.html from origin/main; `sum.cjs` = 11 screen changes, main-thread time + time spent over 33ms per frame; `forced.cjs` = forced style/layout with JS stacks; `ulc.cjs` = elements per style recalc; `swfail.cjs` = Chrome's "Animation" trace events with `compositeFailed`, i.e. which animations couldn't go to the GPU; `layers.cjs` = LayerTree + compositing reasons). Seed `nxEvo: 9` or the first-run evolution power-up pollutes idle traces. Causes found and fixed: (1) `playEnter` toggled `html.enter` with a forced `offsetWidth` on every goTab, and nothing used `html.enter`; because `.panel.enter > :nth-child(n)` exists, a class change on `<html>` is a full-subtree invalidation, so the whole page restyled on add and again on removal 1.2s later: now a no-op. (2) The panel cascade (`.panel.enter` added on show, removed at 900ms) did the same to the panel's subtree: now the first 4 (11 without html.fast) children get the same `pnCascade` as an inline `style.animation` (replaces their own animation like the class did; removed at 1.1s only if still ours), once per switch (paintTab's remove + add arrive as two mutation records: handled per batch). (3) v68's WAAPI tab slide on `.panel.on` on top of the CSS slide (slashin/cut/smR/smL): two transform animations on one element = `TargetHasIncompatibleAnimations`, both on the main thread; removed (CSS slide kept), and its `scrollY` read (forced style inside the tap, 92ms at 4x) moved into a rAF. (4) `lagWatch` (rAF loop for 0.9s after every screen change) uses `long-animation-frame` entries when supported (Chrome; ≥50ms instead of ≥45ms), rAF loop kept for Safari. (5) `flickWatch` samples every 40ms on a timer instead of every frame (it forced 57–139ms of style at 4x per game page open). (6) The view pill highlight `.vslide` animated `width` (layout every frame): last script redefines `slidePillNow` to set the width at once + WAAPI `translateX scaleX(old/new)` from the left (`pill.dataset.bw/bx` remember the last spot), CSS keeps only the opacity transition. Result (3 runs each, sum.cjs): time over 33ms per frame 1939/1862/1496 → 1398/1095/983ms (−34%), main thread 12358 → 11148ms avg (−10%); style recalcs per switch: games 156+92 → one ~70–130ms pass, plan 103+50+36 → 21ms, home 35+70+68 → 56ms. Nexi learned: `gpuCheck(what)` (last script; lagWatch wrapper, 160ms after each screen change, off while touring/calm) reports kind "slow" when one piece ≥64×64 on screen has two running animations on transform or opacity ("two animations move the same piece at once … can't run on the GPU"), or animates width/height/top/left/margin/padding… for ≥200ms or forever ("animates its width … redraws the page every frame"). Planted v260's slide → reported on every switch; planted a 500ms width animation on a panel's first piece → reported; sweeps at 412/859/360 (run alone, not next to a benchmark: CPU contention makes the older lag checks fire) → 0 reports. Tour checked at 412/900. Not done (measured, parked): idle main frames (~1 per frame from small main-thread animations: the countdown digits, Nexi's face transitions on width/height, nav colour transitions; ~2% CPU), bugLayout's idle pass (~80–90ms at 4x per new screen key, in requestIdleCallback), per-element style cost (3515 rules: ~0.1ms/element at 4x).
- Usage-guard replaced by auto-handoff (no app version): the meter, `⛽` reply line, band, status line and 200k/limit nudges are gone (the Claude app's own context popup shows usage); `.claude/skills/auto-handoff/` starts a fresh cloud chat with a handoff note by itself once a chat passes 300k tokens (once per chat, `$.state`), and "handoff" still does it by hand. 3 tests.
- v262: (the owner on v261: "still lag and all the animations have frozen", testing opening the app + the queue). (1) Frozen = v260's rest (12s without a touch pauses every looping animation): `REST_AFTER` = 60s now (Settings note says "a minute"). In Chromium nothing else stayed paused (only Nexi's flame, by design). (2) Phone reports #382–#387 (v259/v260): 340–520ms "rendering" stutters on Queue right after tapping a row. Traced (scratchpad dec.cjs: the main thread's "Decode Image" with its parents): picking a game re-bakes the queue backdrop (`bgBake`) and the live light (`hlvScene`), and drawing an <img> into a canvas makes Chrome decode the whole JPEG on the main thread at `CanvasRenderingContext2D::FinalizeFrame` (50 + 134ms for 1920px heroes at 4x; `im.decode()` doesn't help, canvas decodes again). New `pnSmallBitmap(url, w)` (next to bgBake): fetch → blob → `createImageBitmap(blob, { resizeWidth })`, decoded off the main thread, memo of 24; null on CORS failure / no support, then the old <img> path. bgBake uses 512px, hlvScene 256px (still CORS-clean for hlvColours). Queue row tap at 4x: 9 frames ≥60ms → 4, no main-thread decode. Fresh container was ~50% slower than the v261 one: sum.cjs live 2988/2730 vs new 2868/2651 over-33ms (screen switches unchanged, as expected); 859 sweep gives the same older lag reports on live v261 (8) as on v262 (6), gpuCheck none; 412/360 only "Opening the app froze" (also on live). Tour checked at 412/900. Not a detector case beyond v261's (the lag checks already caught it as "rendering"); a main-thread decode check would need trace access.
- v263: 120Hz at last (the owner after v261/v262: "still lags, the fps counter only shows 60 or 70"). The number was the clue: Chrome on Android draws the main thread at most 60 times a second (main frames are throttled to 60Hz on high-refresh phones; compositor-only animation and scrolling still run at the panel's rate) and Android picks the panel rate from what the app actually draws, so anything that needs the main thread on every frame holds the whole screen at 60. Measured with scratchpad harnesses (40 seeded games, PIL covers/art, external hosts blocked; `soak.cjs` = BeginMainThreadFrame per second per screen from a devtools.timeline.frame trace, headless = 60Hz so 60/s means every frame; `burst.cjs` = per-second main frames + StyleRecalcInvalidationTracking nodes + Animation trace events with compositeFailed; `exp.cjs`/`lockdig.cjs`/`rapiddig.cjs` = run JS steps (cancel groups, remove subtrees) and recount; `launches.cjs` = N cold launches; `rapid.cjs` = 13 screen switches 150–500ms apart, then idle on Home/Queue/game page; `dtest.cjs` = drawCheck planted/sweep). IMPORTANT for anyone measuring idle: seed `restOff: true` (the rest mode pauses loops after 60s since v262, 12s before, and hides most of this) and `pcdOff: true` (a seeded game out recently brings up the release-day card at 2.6s, whose letters animate for ~6s). Old build (v261, rest off, 859 and 412): 28–60 main frames/s on every screen at idle. Causes, all fixed: (1) Home's countdown (`[data-cd-to]`, the 1s ticker) flipped its digits with WAAPI on the hidden Home while you were on any other screen; an animation on something not drawn can't go to the GPU, so it ran on the main thread for 520ms of every second (≈30 frames/s everywhere). A hidden countdown (`checkVisibility({contentVisibilityAuto})`, fallback `.panel:not(.on)`) now only changes its digits. (2) Infinite loops on hidden screens and on skipped `content-visibility: auto` rows (Home's orb/blob/Nexi line blink on a hidden Home, the queue's floaty cover and "Out now" shine, loading shimmers on lazy pictures that never load on hidden screens): `offHold(root)` pauses running infinite loops whose piece isn't drawn (`offSeen`), `offFree(root)` plays them again once drawn (in rAF + 400ms after `.on`: checkVisibility is still false in the mutation callback), cancelling instead when the CSS no longer names that animation (a shimmer whose picture loaded while hidden). Triggers: a MutationObserver on every `.panel` (class on the panel itself = shown/hidden; class/childList inside a hidden one = a redraw, looked at 300ms later), a sweep of all hidden panels 1.5s after any change, a 2s interval sweep of the whole page (`offHold(document.body)` + `offFree(null)`, 1–7ms at 4x CPU; catches warmed-up / late redraws and Settings' chips, whose `pnPick` finished inside closed `<details>` sections: checkVisibility is false there and they left ghosts, Settings sat at 44–60 frames/s until this), a capture `toggle` listener (a section opened frees its loops in the next frame), and `contentvisibilityautostatechange`. CSS can't do this: Chrome doesn't restyle a skipped subtree, so a `.panel:not(.on) * { animation-play-state: paused }` rule never reaches running loops (tried, removed). Rest mode interplay: loops REST holds that are on a hidden screen move to OFFHOLD; freed while resting they go to REST.held. (3) Ghosts: a composited animation that finishes (or was finished) with `fill: both` on a piece that is no longer drawn leaves Chrome's GPU side waiting (trace: NeedsTickAnimations / ProxyMain::SetNeedsAnimate every frame, no style work), main frames at 60 until it's cancelled. Seen after leaving the Queue (pop/statin/pnPip/artin), after a fast switch cut a v261 `pnCascade` short, and when a loading shimmer made finite (tried: `animation-iteration-count: 6`) ran out on a hidden screen. offHold now `finish()`es one-offs cut off by the hide and, finished ones without a fill are simply cancelled (no visual effect); for finished ones holding their last frame, `commitStyles()` + `cancel()` (the piece keeps the exact look; `OFFCOMMIT` remembers what was written and offFree takes each inline value off again when the screen shows, unless removing it would change the computed value, so later CSS changes to the piece still apply; pseudo-element animations can't be committed and are left alone). The shimmer stays infinite (offHold handles it) and `pnLd` cancels a picture's pnShim when it loads (the CSS can't drop it on a hidden screen). (4) Nexi's face: moods changed eye height/top and mouth width/height/borders with transitions (`transition: all .15s`), and a blink restarted next to one ran its whole 4.6s loop on the main thread (seen: 60 frames/s for 4–6s after each mood). Shapes now change at once; the look-around keeps its translate glide. (5) `loopKick` (nudges a loop's delay by .01ms so Chrome hands it to the GPU again) was tried for loops that start during the launch card and was dropped: nudging one that isn't drawn yet is itself what gets it stuck. After (rest off): 2–9 main frames/s on all 13 screens at 412 and 859 (the countdown digit and Nexi's look-around), 6/6 cold launches clean at 412, 8/8 rapid-switch runs clean on Home/Queue/game page; scrolling unchanged (compositor scroll; ~0.4ms of handlers per move at 4x). Nexi learned: `drawCheck` (last script; armed 3s after the last touch/scroll/key, 15s after launch, off while touring/calm/resting/dragging, at most 6 per session): `drawLook` samples `document.getAnimations()` 8 times 250ms apart; a running animation counts if its piece isn't drawn, or it's visible, lasts ≥400ms (or loops) and animates a property only the main thread can draw (anything but transform/translate/rotate/scale/opacity/filter); seen in ≥3 samples in two looks 3s apart → kind "slow": "Something hidden keeps animating on X (…): it can't run on the GPU, so the screen is held at 60Hz" or "A piece on X animates its P (…), which only the main processor can draw, so the screen is held at 60Hz". Planted: an infinite background-position loop on Home → reported; a 1s WAAPI flip inside a `content-visibility: hidden` box → reported; v261's countdown put back (plantC.html) → reported on Queue and Month. Sweep (dtest.cjs, 13 screens each at 412/859/360, rest off): 0 reports of any kind. Parked: during scroll every main frame restyles all ~50 running animated pieces (composited loops still get "Animation" style invalidations when a main frame happens anyway): ~4ms per frame at 4x.
- Tour-only fix after v263 (no version; #391 "Tour step v259 can't find what it points at", `.dzd-row`): the On deck step pointed only at the v259 photo cards, which don't exist when On deck cards are set to Classic, On deck is switched off, or nothing is on deck. It now falls back to `#p-queue .deck` (Classic strip), then `#p-queue .tabbar` (top of the Queue). Its `go` and the Insights step's used `setSub`, which doesn't redraw (opened from Library, the step pointed into the hidden Queue): both use `goSub` now. Test (scratchpad t391.cjs, app opened on Library, New/Classic/off × 412/859): before New found / Classic none / off none; after dzd-row / deck / tabbar, all visible on the Queue. tourat4.cjs: steps v259+ all found from Library and Played.
- v264: the GPU side at last (the owner, the fifth time: "still lags"; no phone reports from v263, because every lag check watches the main thread). Read the FPS counter right: 60 *or 70* is not a hard 60Hz cap, it's a pipeline that finishes ~65 frames a second with an idle main thread, i.e. the GPU. New harnesses (scratchpad, lost between chats): `quads.cjs` = Chromium with `--enable-gpu --use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader --enable-gpu-rasterization`, Tracing via `browser.startTracing` with `disabled-by-default-viz.quads` + `disabled-by-default-cc.debug(.quads)`; the last `cc::LayerTreeHostImpl` object snapshot (`ph: "O"`) has `frame.render_passes[].quad_list` (each quad's `visible_rect_as_target_space_quad`, `should_draw_with_blending`, `shared_state.id_ref` → `shared_quad_state_list[].layer_id`, ids are `viz::SharedQuadState/0x…`, compare the part after `/`) and `active_tree.layers[].layer_name` (the DOM element); `lay2.py` adds each layer's area clipped to its `clip_rect` and the pass `output_rect`, over the viewport = "screens drawn per frame". `fps.cjs` = rAF frames/s per screen on that software GPU (a proxy for a GPU-bound phone; 4 cores, so run nothing next to it). `look.cjs` = same moment in old/new (CSS animations: `updateTiming({delay: 0})`, `pause()`, `currentTime`; the new loop: `sc._f.clock`, `l.off = 0`, `hlvfDraw`), specks hidden, screenshots diffed (`cmp.py`). `inv.cjs` = a rAF check before every paint (backdrop drawn twice / missing / flat mid-fade / wrong canvas) through tab switches, 6 queue picks, game page, backdrop off/on, Motion off, fold/unfold, light off/on. Seed for any sweep: `tourFrom: +V, tourRedo: 1` or the 96-update catch-up tour runs and every check is off (`nxtouring`). Found: on Games/Plan/Taste ~5.5–5.8 screens of BLENDED layers per frame (with the page's opaque background ~6.5–7): `#bgart` canvas (.58), `#bgtint`, `.hlv-art` (128%), three `.hlv-l` (76vmax), the page; hiding #hlv/#bgart/#bgtint → 0.7–1.1. Something always moves (lights, specks, Nexi), so all of it is re-blended at the panel rate on ~5MP. Fix (last `<style>`/`<script>`, `hlvf*`): `hlvScene` is wrapped; each new scene's art + 3 lights move into ONE canvas `.hlv-f` at ¼ of the screen (keeps v257's `.hlv-s canvas` rule: own layer), redrawn every 33ms from a timer with v257's paths in JS (`HLVF_KF` = hlvArt/hlvL1–3 keyframes, `hlvfEase` = cubic-bezier(.42,0,.58,1) per keyframe segment, art 48s alternate, light duration/negative delay read from their inline style; `html.still` = untransformed). When one scene has settled on #hlv and one baked backdrop shows with nothing fading (`hlvfBaseFor`: bgon+hlvon+fast, no `getAnimations()` on the scene or #bgart/#bgart2, filter none, identity transform, tint a plain top-to-bottom gradient parsed by `hlvfStops` from its computed style), the backdrop (at its opacity) and the tint are drawn into the bottom of that canvas and `html.hlvflat` hides #bgart/#bgart2/#bgtint (`html.hlvflat.hlvon.bgon …` so losing a class shows them again by CSS alone). "Over" is associative, so the pixels match. `hlvfExit` (base out, canvas redrawn, class off, same task) runs from wrappers on hlvShow/hlvHideAll/hlvSync, a MutationObserver on #bgart/#bgart2/#bgtint (style, class, children), #full (class) and <html> (class/style, only when bgon/hlvon/fast/still or the style attribute changed), and resize; a check every 8th tick (or right after an exit) puts it back. Holds still (no clock) while resting (`pnRest`), dragging, hidden or Motion off; stops when no live scene. Results: blended screens per frame old → new Plan 5.5 → 2.7, Taste 5.6 → 2.7, Queue 5.1 → 3.1, Home 4.2 → 3.4, game page 3.5 → 2.6 (the rest is the page itself); software-GPU frames/s (2 runs) Plan 26/24 → 39/37, Taste 25/26 → 39/40, Queue 26/25 → 31/33, Home 31/30 → 36/34, game page 38/36 → 47/49. Look: same moment old vs new, mean difference 0.1–0.3/255 on all 5 screens, the only spots >8 are Nexi's eyes (random glance). Invariants: 0 bad frames in ~2400. Main-thread cost: one ¼-size canvas draw per 33ms (5 drawImage + 1 gradient), ~0.1ms. Nexi learned: `stackLook` / `stackCheck` (last script): 3.5s after the last touch/scroll/key or tab switch, once per screen+size, ≤6 a session, off while touring: adds up the on-screen area of see-through pieces with their own layer (canvas/video, `position: fixed`, will-change transform/opacity; ≥45%×30% of the screen, ≥25% visible; must paint something; a backdrop blur counts ×3; only what follows the last solid full-screen piece in document order, e.g. a game's page), twice 3s apart, ≥3 both times → kind "slow" "N screens of see-through layers are stacked on X (…)". Old build scores 3.3–4.4 on Queue/Plan/Taste/Home (would report), new 1.0; planted 3 fixed gradient layers on Insights → reported (4.0). The first `stackLook` walked all ~3000 elements and itself made a 130–140ms stutter (offsetWidth on rows skipped by content-visibility forces their layout): it now walks only outside `.wrap` (depth ≤5) plus `.wrap`'s screens two levels deep, and fully into a fixed overlay kept in `.wrap` (`#full` lives there), with `checkVisibility({contentVisibilityAuto})` pruning: 3–19ms at 4x CPU. It also skips while `#sheet` is open (a passing overlay: Settings over a game page is sheet + page + light = 3.0). Also found in the sweep: a scene v257 started before the last script loaded (the parser lets frames run) finished un-adopted (four layers again), so the `hlvShow` wrapper adopts too. Sweep (`sweep.cjs`, 17 screens: home, games×4, plan×4, taste×5, add, a game page, Settings; 8s each) at 412/360: 0 reports of any kind; at 859: 0 stack reports, two from the older lag checks ("Opening the app froze for 300ms", "Switching to Played stuttered, worst 80ms") — live v263 at 859 in the same container gives the same kind ("Switching to Insights stuttered, worst 80ms"). Scores: every tab screen 1.0, Add 2.0, game page 2.0, Settings 3.0 (skipped). Tour (S.lastV 263) at 412/900: both v264 steps, on Plan then Home, card in view. Also noted for the owner: the in-app FPS counter is itself a rAF loop, i.e. it counts main-thread frames.
- v265 (the owner after v264: "every time you fix it, it still lags … it lags everywhere, as if it's too much: a fast scroll up or down on the Queue, sideways on a game's page, switching tabs a lot"; asked for the on-phone measurement). (1) Blocking touch listeners: `.panel:not(#p-home)` touchmove (drag-to-reorder) and every Queue row's touchmove had `passive: false`, so at the start of every swipe on 8 screens Chrome had to wait for the main thread before scrolling (Android: a non-passive touchmove makes the compositor wait on the first move of each sequence; busy app = the swipe stands still). Both are passive now; while a held row is lifted, `dragBlock` (last script, wraps `startDrag`; removes itself when `drag` is null, plus a touchend/touchcancel check) is the only blocking listener. Runtime listener census (scratchpad lis.cjs: `EventTarget.prototype.addEventListener` wrapped in an init script; element touch listeners without `passive` default to blocking, window/document/html/body default passive): v264 8 panels + 80 rows, v265 none. NOTE for testing: in this container's Chromium, CDP touch input waits for the main thread even with zero blocking listeners (touch2/touch3.cjs: a 400ms busy main thread delayed the first scroll step ~410–540ms in both builds, with or without GPU flags), so swipe-blocking can't be shown here; only the census proves it. Drag test (drag.cjs, CDP touch: hold 520ms, 14 moves of ¼ row): v264 and v265 both lift the row and land it 3 places down. (2) v264's `hlvfDraw` read `innerWidth/innerHeight` for 76vmax: that forces a full style+layout when the page has changes, so during a game-page swipe every 33ms redraw forced one (hubtr.cjs at 4x: ~80ms forced inside `hlvfTick` over one swipe). Uses `HLVF.W/H` (resize) now. And the loop holds still (`HLVF.busy`) while a finger is down, 400ms after it lifts, 300ms after a scroll, 900ms after goTab/goSub/openFull (no clock advance, no backdrop checks; carries on from the same spot). (3) The game page's swipe to the next game: measured (4x): drag start restyles ~900 elements once (27ms), then ~21–26 elements per frame (2–4ms); not the main cost on a phone, left as is. (4) The smoothness check (`prb*`, last script; the owner: "yes build it"): once per APP_V, on the phone, during normal use, times rAF gaps during vertical scrolls, sideways scrolls (and `hubPaint`, the swipe to the next game), switches (goTab/goSub) and game-page opens; drops = Σ(round(gap/refresh) − 1), refresh read from the 15th percentile gap (120/90/60); long animation frames during the moment are added up. Turns `PRB_ARMS` = base, no background (`prb-nobg`: #hlv/#hlvg/#bgart*/#bgtint/#pnorbs hidden), base, no shadows, base, no blurs/filters, base, no animations (CSS paused + hlvf frozen), base; a turn ends at ≥4 moments and 12s, or 9 moments. State in localStorage `gq:probe` (not S). Toast once at the start ("timing how smooth…; some decorations will switch off for a little while") and at the end. Report: kind "slow", sigAt "probe vN" (one issue per version), msg = drops per kind normally + change in points with each suspect off; stack (≤8 lines) = per-turn table + pictures loaded (count, total MP, largest), elements, animations, cores, memory. End to end in Chromium (probet.cjs: touch swipes on the Queue, 4 tab switches, game page + sideways swipe): all 9 turns, both toasts, report filed (synthetic data showed 158MP of decoded pictures: watch that line on the phone). Nexi learned: a wrapper on `EventTarget.prototype.addEventListener` (last script; catches what's added after load, e.g. re-rendered rows) looks 1.5s later at any non-passive touchstart/touchmove (except `dragBlock`): still connected, touch-action not none, ≥¼ of the screen or inside a scroller (the page counts when it scrolls) → kind "slow" "A touchmove listener that isn't passive sits on X …", once per place a session. Planted v264's two (panel touchmove passive:false, row touchstart without options) → both reported; a real drag + re-render → nothing.
- v266: the self-test, step 1 of the plan the owner agreed to (the owner after v265: "I swiped fast and the same issue: on the Queue the games just disappear for a moment, and the game hub lags after 4–5 games. Don't start fixing, I want a full solution, we're in a loop"). Phone reports #401–#408 (v265, 859×847 @2.63): Queue frames of 100–470ms with script ≤16ms and style+layout 0ms, i.e. drawing. Root-cause hypothesis (unproven until the phone says so): pictures. Since v215 (#84, `S.picHQ`) every picture loads at full size; Queue rows' `.cardbg` (cardLayers, `S.cardBg` "hero" default) use `bgSource(g)` = the hero (Steam `library_hero.jpg` is 3840×1240) shown faded behind a ~860px row; game pages hold 4K hero + key art + logo, and `hubPage` builds the neighbours with `decoding = "sync"`. The container never runs out of GPU/image memory, which is why four rounds measured here missed it. The plan: (1) measure on the phone (this release), (2) every picture at the size it's shown × DPR, (3) at most current/prev/next game pages, prepared in quiet moments, pictures released when left, (4) Nexi watches picture memory, (5) the same self-test after, "done" only on the phone's numbers. Self-test (`st*`, last script): Settings → This build → `#s-stest` "⏱ Run the smoothness self-test" (button in the template next to `#s-whatsnew`). ~22s, a touch stops it (still reports, marked "stopped early"). `stPics(root)`: every `img` (currentSrc) and every `url(...)` in a style attribute (incl. `--bgimg`), real size via `new Image()` (size without decode) vs box × DPR; MP held, MP needed, how many ≥3000px, largest. `stFling`: `scrollTo({behavior: "smooth"})` bottom, top, bottom (compositor scroll), rAF gaps + LoAF ms per leg. `stSwipe`: `hubDragStart` → `hd.dx` to −0.6W over 240ms with `hubPaint` → `hubDragEnd(1.2)` (exactly a finger's path; `hubStep` when hub motion is off), 10 times from the first game, 900ms each, then `stHold()` = #full elements, `.hub` pages, `.hlv-s` scenes, animations. Card (dyShow, `.st-r`/`.st-n`) + report kind "slow", sigAt "selftest vN #k" (run count in localStorage `gq:stest`). Quiet while it runs: a BUG_MODAL window, `window.pnSelfTest` (prbOk ignores it; a `bugReport` wrapper drops other "slow" reports until 1.5s after it puts the app back, because lagWatch doesn't read BUG_MODAL). Tested (scratchpad stt.cjs/stt2.cjs, Settings button tapped, 859 @2.63 and 412): 22s, back on the starting screen, card shown, one report queued and nothing else; touch at 9s → stopped early, partial report. Synthetic data already shows the shape: Queue 51 MP held (205 MB decoded) for 6 MP on screen; light scenes 4 → 9 over 10 swipes (accumulating). Tour at 412/900: opens Settings on the button.
- v267: pictures at the size they're shown (step 2–4 of the agreed plan). The phone's v266 self-test (#411, #413, both runs alike): Queue 99 pictures = 372 MP (1488 MB unpacked) for 14 MP on screen, 14 at 4K, the largest 70 MP; fling 51–53% frames dropped, worst 600ms with a 600ms long animation frame (the main thread stuck behind unpacking); game page swipes 9–10 up to 45% / 538ms; light scenes 3→8, #full elements 175→189. (1) sw.js layer: `IMG` = play-next-img-3 (img-2 held full-size copies; activate deletes it). Every cross-origin `destination: "image"` request (IMG_HOST no-cors as before, plus any https host, no-cors or cors) that isn't already cached goes through `fitFetch`: `https://wsrv.nl/?url=…&w=2560&h=2560&fit=inside&we&output=webp&q=86` (9s abort), cached under the original address, so markup, CSS, `new Image()`, `decode()` (warmGame, hubReady, the cover loading bar) all get the screen-sized copy; `FIT_SKIP` (Steam header/capsule/library_600x900/logo.png, svg, gif) and wsrv itself go straight through; a failed/slow copy falls back to the original. (2) Page layer (`FIT`, `fit*`, last script): a MutationObserver (childList + src/style attributes on <html>) queues every `img` and every element with `url(` in its style; one rAF reads all boxes, then writes: a piece drawn smaller than 1536 device px on both sides gets `fitSized(u, w, h)` = wsrv `w/h` rounded up to STEPS (128…1536) with `fit=outside&we` (covers its box, never enlarges); data:/blob: uploads get a phone-made copy (`fitLocal`: createImageBitmap with resizeWidth/Height, canvas → webp blob). Not drawn yet (hidden screen, content-visibility skipped, 0×0) → `FIT.wait`, retried on goTab/goSub/openFull, `contentvisibilityautostatechange` and 150ms after a scroll. Each piece remembers `_fitFrom`/`_fitTo`/`_fitN` (refits from the original when its box grows: fold → unfold) and `_fitSig` (its picture addresses), so style changes that aren't pictures (transform every frame) are skipped. A copy that errors puts the original back and marks it `FIT.bad`. (3) Nexi learned (`picLook`/`picCheck`, PIC): 4s after a screen settles (once per screen+size, ≤6 a session, off while touring or self-testing), visible loaded <img>s: one over 8 MP and 4× its spot, or the screen over 60 MP and 4× what it shows → kind "slow". Tests (scratchpad; pictures from fake.steamstatic.com routed to the scratchpad files incl. a 9600×7200 giant.jpg; wsrv.nl routed to fakewsrv.py, a PIL stand-in with request_queue_size 512; IMPORTANT: run with PW_EXPERIMENTAL_SERVICE_WORKER_NETWORK_EVENTS=1, the SW claims the page on the first load and its fetches skip context.route otherwise; goto with waitUntil domcontentloaded): self-test v266 vs v267 at 859 @2.63 (stx.cjs): Queue 112 MP (448 MB) → 16 MP (64 MB), largest 69 → 4.2 MP, 4K 4 → 0, game page 8.8 → 3.9 MP, worst swipe frame 700ms → 58ms (fling in Chromium was already fine: no GPU here). fitchk.cjs: folded 412 a 78×92 cover → 256 copy; unfolded 859 the same → 384, the big card 768 → 1536; wsrv answering 503 → every picture back to its original, none broken; planted 69 MP <img> in a 200×150 box → "A 69.1 MP picture fills a 0.2 MP spot on Queue". Screenshot old/new Queue: same pictures, same sharpness. Expect on the phone: the first time each picture shows after the update it comes through the resize service once (the old full-size cache is gone), then it's kept. WebKit check (first push) caught `picCheck` on iPad: "A 12 MP picture fills a 0 MP spot on Add/Home" (the app's own files, served from its own address, weren't fitted: `fitRemote` skipped http and same-origin). Same-origin files (`fitSame`: our origin or a bare relative name) now go through `fitLocal` like uploads; `fitLocal` reads the size from `new Image()` (header, no decode) and unpacks once with resizeWidth/Height; a failure marks `FIT.bad`. localfit.cjs (seed.cjs pictures from localhost, 810×1080 @2): Home 18 pictures / 17 resized, Add 5/5, Games 52/51, biggest left 2.1 MP, no reports.
- v268 (the owner sent the v267 self-test card: Queue fling 4% dropped / worst 33ms (v266: 53% / 600ms), swipes 1–3 4%, 8–10 2% (v266 6% / 14%), Queue pictures 113 MB for 54 MB shown (v266 1488 MB), 0 at 4K; then: "but now game pictures and art take longer to load, sometimes I wait 3 seconds for its info and art"). Cause: v267's sw.js sent every new picture through wsrv.nl (it downloads the original itself, then resizes; ~1.5s+ a picture, far away), and layer 2 (`fitSized`, index.html) asks wsrv for each box size separately (cover at 128, the same cover at 384 on a game page, the strip at 1536×192…), each a new trip, plus the original request on the same element = 2–3 service trips per picture. (1) sw.js does it on the phone now: `baseCopy` fetches the original from its own site (CORS, as before v267), `picSize` reads width × height from the first 64 KB (PNG IHDR, JPEG SOFn, WebP VP8/VP8L/VP8X), keeps it as is if ≤ CAP (2560) else `shrink` (createImageBitmap + OffscreenCanvas, high smoothing, webp .86; Safari can't write webp → JPEG, or PNG when the source may be see-through). A layer-2 request (its wsrv.nl address with `fit=outside`, intercepted, never sent) is `sizedCopy`: cut from the kept copy, no network. `once(key)` shares one job per address (the original and its sized copy asked at once = one download); `fitSlot` unpacks ≤2 at a time. wsrv only as a fallback (`viaService`, raced against 1.5s: slower → the original shows now and the service's copy is kept for next time): sites that refuse CORS, no OffscreenCanvas, a picture that won't unpack. Same IMG cache (img-3; its wsrv copies stay valid). (2) Self-test card compares itself: last full run in localStorage `gq:stestlast` (not S), "was …" under each number (`.st-was`), a verdict line (`.st-v`: smoother / rougher / about the same by fling + the worse end of the swipes, ±3 points, naming both), and the report gets a "last run" line; before any stored run, Android compares with the v266 phone run (`ST_BASE`, #413). (3) Nexi learned (`picWait`, last script; goTab/openFull wrapped): pictures in view that aren't loaded yet when a screen or game page opens are timed (a `new Image()` sharing the page's download); one over 2.5s → kind "slow" "A picture took 3.1s to show on …", once per screen, ≤4 a session, not while touring/self-testing/offline. Tests (scratchpad, lost between chats): pic.cjs = the app at 859×847 @2.63 against fake Steam/SteamGridDB hosts (scratchpad img/: 12× 3840×1240 heroes, 2560×1440 art, 1200×1800 grids, PIL, CDN 120ms) and a fake wsrv (fakewsrv.py, PIL, routed with a 1.5s delay), `PW_EXPERIMENTAL_SERVICE_WORKER_NETWORK_EVENTS=1`, reload once so the SW controls; times every picture request after an action via resource timing. v267 → v268: first Queue all pictures by 6.2s with 10 still missing → 1.1s, 16 → 0 service trips; first game page 3.0s → 0.5s; 2nd new game 1.9s → 0.8s; warm opens equal (20–60ms). Memory kept (mem.cjs, stPics): Queue 14.7 → 13.0 MP held for 5.4 shown, 4K 1 → 0, largest kept 4.8 → 3.7 MP. `.github/scripts/swcheck.mjs` (new step in webkit-check.yml, which now also runs on sw.js) runs sw.js's own picSize + shrink in a WebKit worker on a made-up 3840×1240 JPEG and a see-through PNG (Chromium dry run: 2560×827 webp, strip 1536×496, thumb 594×192, PNG kept see-through).
- v269: the tidy-up (the owner, after asking "rebuild from zero or carry on?" and being told to clean up in place: "do what you like to make this better and smarter and easier for you … organise codes, merge them, cut what is not needed"). No new feature. (1) Test kit in the repo, not the scratchpad: `tools/kit.mjs` (`syntax` / `sweep` / `tour` / `perf` / `all`, seed in `tools/seed.mjs`), and `.github/workflows/app-check.yml` runs `all` on every PR. (2) `tools/map.mjs` writes `docs/MAP.md` (152 blocks, every wrapped function and its layers) and `--dead` lists unused code. (3) Cut: pickDuel, duelFixtures/duelNext/duelFix, nxPoint, lfClear, duelCount, hrsGuess, scoreTxt, lookPlace, pxSeen, an unused genre tally in Taste, and CSS for things no longer drawn (.homehead/.hh-top, .tile.tinted, .upnext, .ringtile, .cdtile, .pk1, .gorow, .dr-h, .fpin/.vtog/.vsep/.z-cov/.z-art/.dcn in shared lists). (4) onNav: the seven v249–v267 hooks that wrapped goTab/goSub/openFull (lagWatch, hlvKick, stackArm, prbStart, hold, fitRetry, picArm) register with one hub (own script just before the v249 one, so the older wraps keep their order); openFull 16 → 10 layers, goTab 11 → 4, goSub 6 → 3; v268's picWait wraps (merged from main meanwhile) became an onNav hook too. Before hooks run newest first, after hooks in order, as the wraps did (checked by spying every hook on old vs new: same calls, same order); a throwing hook is re-thrown in a timeout instead of breaking the screen change. (5) v246's press-animation stripper (walked every CSS rule on each start; missed style blocks after it) is gone: the 50 `:active` rules lost their movement in the source (46 deleted whole). Checked by dumping every CSS rule in Chromium, old (stripped at runtime) vs new: only the removed dead rules and the emptied `:active` rules differ. `kit.mjs syntax` fails on a press effect (planted: the old file gives 50). Sweep 412/859/360 + tour: 0 errors, 0 reports.
- Tidy-up, round 2 (no version: nothing on screen changes). Every remaining wrap of goTab / goSub / openFull (14: usage counts, bugSeen, the v203 openAt, prices, the story so far, reviews + "seen", Library fit, dust, the flying cover, screenshots) and all 9 of openSettings became hooks: the onNav hub moved from its own v249-era script into the core script (just before the first old wrap, so the order is the same), `onSettings` added beside it, and a "before" hook may return a function that runs in the after phase (dust and the flying cover look before and act after). Hooks don't run for `openSettings.tplOnly` (the search index), as every old wrap checked. Sweep/tour/perf: 0 errors, 0 reports, perf and timing notes within noise (859: 8/6 vs 8/6 notes, same kinds).
- Learn mod, where.mjs and kit additions (no app version; merged after v269, whose tidy-up added tools/kit.mjs + map.mjs; this chat had built a parallel kit and folded it in instead of keeping two). (1) `.claude/skills/learn/`, a fourth Claude Code mod, with `tools/learn.mjs` and `docs/LESSONS.md`: a short retrospective at the end of a turn that had a correction, a check that failed then passed, 3 failures of one command, edits that didn't apply, a release-guard stop, a release (APP_V commit / merge), a fix comment without the detector line, or (with a commit) something put off; mistakes and missed opportunities recorded in git (the same lesson again → ×N; ×3 → make it a check and `promote`); lessons and the wrappers of the function being edited shown again when that code is edited or its command runs; the first message knows the counts, ×3 lessons, stale ones, open ideas and auto-bug issues reopened after a fix ("Happened again after it was closed"); a scratchpad Playwright harness → one nudge toward tools/scenarios; a commit carrying index.html reruns `node tools/map.mjs` (docs/MAP.md stays current) and reports new `where.mjs --check` hazards; `/lessons`, `/retro`. Design from research (Reflexion, ExpeL, accumulated behavioral rules from review, Kage's anchored memory, Cursor-style file-triggered rules): lessons cite anchors and come back only where they apply, repeats become checks, plain markdown in git. Loaded live in a headless session (note seen; the Stop retrospective asked once and the model rightly recorded nothing for a made-up correction). (2) `tools/where.mjs`: acorn-parsed, scope-aware queries over index.html's scripts: `<name>` (definition + every later wrapper), `--at=<line>`, `--check [--base=<ref>]` for new v198 (state on a wrapped function), v216 (assigning a top-level const), v237 (calling a function scoped inside another script) and v269 (wrapping goTab/goSub/openFull instead of onNav) hazards; each planted and caught, 0 new on v269. (3) `tools/kit.mjs`: the sweep waits 2.6s per screen (the layout check runs 1.6s after a screen change, when idle: a planted cut-off button was reported on 3 screens with v269's 1.4s, on 10 with 2.6s; the same mistake this chat's first kit made); `--plant x.js --expect`; `run tools/scenarios/x.mjs` with real CDP touch (`p.tap`, `p.swipe`); the test browser has swipe-back off (a right swipe on a game page went "back" to about:blank). `tools/scenarios/swipe-games.mjs`: g1 → g2 → g1 by finger at 412/859. (4) release-guard counts `node tools/kit.mjs sweep|tour|perf|all|run` as a browser test (new test; its merge test, broken by a test-kit change, fixed); auto-handoff records lessons before handing off. Tests: learn 13, release-guard 10, auto-handoff 3, bug-inbox 4.

- v270: iPhones buzz too. Safari has no `navigator.vibrate`; on Apple devices the last script now defines it as "flip a hidden `<input type=checkbox switch>` once per buzz in the pattern" (iOS 18+ gives a light tap on each flip, even from `label.click()`; the trick from lochie/web-haptics), so all 22 existing buzz calls and the Library "Buzz" setting work unchanged; Android keeps the real vibrate. The flip's click/input/change are stopped at window capture so no "tap outside closes it" handler or problem check sees them. Tested: `tools/scenarios/iphone-haptics.mjs` (fake iPhone in Chromium: one flip per buzz, nothing heard) and a probe in `.github/scripts/webkitcheck.mjs` (real WebKit). Also 9 more outside skills (motion: 5 from emilkowalski/skills + fixing-motion-performance; ios-safari-quirks; diagnosing-bugs; find-skills), see CLAUDE.md "Outside skills" (and `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` for `npx skills find`).
- v271: first round of the new animation skills (the owner: "smoothness is rule number one, but I do like animations"). `tools/scenarios/motion-audit.mjs` lists, for every open/close/switch, the finite animations that start within 120ms ("— jumps" = nothing moved): every sheet, game page, chat, peek, toast, tab and sub already moved; the gaps were in-place redraws. Picked with the find-animation-opportunities gate (not 100+/day, a named purpose, ≤300ms UI, transform/opacity only): (1) Month ‹ ›: the title, `.cmonth` and `.clist` slide in from the side you went (28px + fade, 260ms PN_EASE, 30ms apart; calm() = fade only). A capture click listener + rAF after renderCal's own onclick, no wrap. (2) Game page Your progress: an `onNav("after")` hook remembers each game's lit level (`.gd-st.done/.now`, in memory); when the same game's page redraws further along (start playing, finish, drop), the line draws from the old step to the new one and each new dot pops (scale .4→1.3→1). The bar `.gd-fill` (dim track) + `.gd-fbar` (scaleX) is prepended INSIDE the starting `.gd-st`: appended to `.gd-trk` it became the last child, so `.gd-st:last-child::before` stopped matching and Finished's line ran to the edge. The segment's own lines go transparent while `.gd-go` is on; cleanup runs on finish or cancel. Rejected: tab/sub switches (100+/day, and v261 removed the cascade for speed), a calendar swipe (the document-wide tab swipe owns sideways swipes), drawing the line on every game open (tens/day). Perf: 6 alternating `kit.mjs perf` runs with/without the CSS, style 3188 vs 3191 ms (noise); a `.gd-fill > span` rule was swapped for a class anyway (a tag-keyed rule is checked against every span). Check: `tools/scenarios/motion-v271.mjs`.

Found, not changed: the phone reports 60Hz in every self-test (Chrome gets 60 frames a second there, a phone setting); the moving background costs ~5 pts of scroll smoothness there (GPU, can't be seen headless); a big box (over 1536 device px, e.g. Queue's hero `.cardbg` unfolded) keeps a repo-file or uploaded picture at full size (sw.js caps only other sites' pictures), not hit on the owner's Queue (largest 2.1 MP). `kit.mjs all` clear.

Grumble's save reaction does nothing while he's hidden. (1) Bug finder `tools/lint.mjs` (ESLint correctness rules over all 105 script blocks with the 1,370 shared top-level names as globals, plus sw.js and the helper), counted by the audit (`lint`, budget 0) so every PR runs it (app-check installs eslint/globals/acorn in the same npm call as Playwright; node_modules/ is git-ignored). (2) `extend(name, { before, after, map })` at the top of the core script, `EXT[name]` lists the extensions; renderHome, applyLook, syncPlayed (6 wraps each), paintTab, pnBubSay (5), pnBubLines, openEdit (4) converted, so no function is wrapped 4+ times any more (wraps 127 → 92). - v280: still while you move (the owner: "what do we have and how can we improve the smoothness, it lags less but still does it a lot, and how can we ensure it stops"). Measured at 859 (`tools/scenarios/trace-scroll.mjs`, `trace-style.mjs`): a finger scroll makes Chrome draw a main-thread frame every frame, and each one restyles every running endless animation even though they run on the GPU (Nexi's blink/breath/blob/core + the rail's live strip: 315 style passes over six Queue swipes). With the phone's own `prb-noanim` arm: scroll style 143 → 42ms (Queue), 154 → 49ms (Library); shadows and blurs only ~15%. Matches the phone's v269 smoothness check (#438): sideways swipes 15% of frames dropped, 3% with animations off. Disproved first: the scroll lag checker's rAF loop (`tick`, v198) asks for 126 of 151 frames but switching it off changed nothing (Chrome draws a main frame per scroll frame anyway). Fix (last script, `mvh*`): from the first touchmove/scroll/wheel until 0.5s after the last one, every running endless animation is paused (spinners in `REST_KEEP` keep going; a touch on `#pnbub` doesn't hold Nexi), loops that start mid-move are caught by `animationstart`; on release each plays again unless the hidden-screen hold (`OFFHOLD`, handed over when its element isn't drawn any more), rest mode (`REST.held`) or the rail's own touch pause (`RLV.hold/drag`, `rlvCalm`) holds it. After: Queue scroll 100 style passes / 16ms (was 315 / 104ms), trace-scroll style 132 → 65ms, prepaint 61 → 44ms; screen changes never start the hold; `kit perf` same within noise. Guard: `node tools/kit.mjs smooth` (in `all`, so every PR): mid-swipe on Queue, Library and a game page at 412/859 no endless animation may be running (the old build fails all three at 859: 7–9 loops) and a vertical scroll must leave them running again. Audit: `subtreeObservers` no longer counts `getAnimations({ subtree: true })` (6 real observers, budget lowered 7 → 6). For the owner (code-check issue): the touch glow (`pnrip`, v68) still spreads under the finger on every row/tile/chip at the start of each scroll (forced layout + a 2.2× row-size layer), despite #309; and a game page at 859 runs 25 endless loops at rest.
- v280, part 2 (the owner: "remove the touch glow and any touch effects when I touch an empty spot"): (1) v68's touch glow is removed (`.pnrip` + `@keyframes pnRip` + its document pointerdown listener, which also measured the row and read its computed position on every finger-down). (2) Sticky touch looks: phones keep `:hover` on whatever was touched last, so a Queue row turned grey and stayed grey after a scroll started on it, the gear stayed lit, etc. All 8 `:hover` rules (#gear, .sortpop button, .calarrow, .row.artrow > .rowbg, .icon, .row, .hit, .dxres button) now sit in `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` (mouse only; `:focus-visible` and `:active` colour parts kept as they were). Guards: `kit.mjs syntax` fails on a `:hover` rule outside `@media (hover: hover)` (caught all 8 on v279); `tools/scenarios/touch-empty.mjs` taps empty spots on Home/Queue/Library/Plan/Taste/a game page/Settings at 412/859 with real touch (clicks swallowed) and fails on any animation, transition, added element or changed look, on anything drawn into a touched row/tile/chip, and on a `:hover` rule outside the media query incl. script-added CSS (v279 fails: glow on chips + the 8 rules; v280: nothing). Not touch effects, left: Nexi's eyes following the finger (kept in v274), `:active` colour changes on buttons (#309 kept them). (3) The hold, reworked in the same release: a script `pause()` takes a CSS animation away from CSS for good, so Nexi's flame (paused by CSS until `.ssj`) would run hidden after a power-up, and a loop CSS switches on mid-move fires no animationstart (the 859 game-page swipe caught it). CSS loops are now held by a class on their element (`.mvh`, `animation-play-state: paused !important` incl. ::before/::after, removed on release so CSS is in charge again); script loops (the rail strip) keep pause()/play(); during a move `mvhHold` re-runs at most every 200ms (16ms of script over six swipes). `kit smooth` gained "power-up mid-swipe" (859): the flame held mid-swipe, running after, paused by CSS when the power-up ends (the pause() version fails it: 17 loops mid-swipe). Queue scroll after both parts: 10 style passes / 3ms (v279: 315 / 104ms); trace-scroll style 132 → 21ms, raster 208 → 24ms, draw 205 → 120ms. Nexi (`#pnbub`) is held whole (`#pnbub.mvh *`): swiping to the next game makes him react (pnTalk, the flame) by class mid-swipe, which the 200ms re-hold left running for a moment (seen 3/3 in `kit smooth` at 859). The kit's smooth phones set `S.nxEvo = 99` so the 15s "I evolved" timer can't land mid-test. `important` budget 167 → 168: `.mvh` must beat Nexi's `animation: … !important` rules. The hold rule carries two IDs' weight (`html:not(#mvh0):not(#mvh1) .mvh`, `html:not(#mvh0) #pnbub.mvh *`): GitHub's app-check caught Nexi's charge shake (`#pnbub.ssj-charge .core { animation: ssjShake … }` with one ID, forced) still running mid-swipe; `kit smooth`'s power-up case now also samples Nexi during the charge.
